Karasuno clawed back 2, a Tsukishima block and a Kiyose pass that let Asahi swing, and at 23-24 Ubugawa served the last ball at Kiyose, a big jump serve. He passed it, a good pass, to Kageyama, and Kageyama set Hinata, and Hinata, eyes open, hit it into the block, and it came down on Karasuno's side.

23-25.

It was Hinata's ball, and nobody's fault, and they went up the hill.

---

In the 2nd game, against Shinzen, at 8-12, after Hinata's 4th eyes-open swing of the game had gone into the net and Kageyama had looked at the ceiling, Ukai called a timeout and banned the new quick.

He did it standing in the middle of the huddle, and he did it properly.

"Listen. The new quick. The 1 where he's got his eyes open and you're trying to stop it at the top." He looked at Kageyama, then at Hinata. "It's not ready. You both know it's not ready. In practice, every night, as much as you like. In a game, no. Not until it works in practice 7 times out of 10."

Hinata opened his mouth.

"You'll get it back," said Ukai, before he could. "When you've earned it. Until then, in games, normal quicks. 1st tempo. Hinata, you go when the ball's in Kageyama's hands, the way you used to. Kageyama, you set it where he is. That still works. It worked in May." He pointed at the court. "You lose a game here, you run a hill. You lose a game at the Spring High, you go home. I'd rather you ran the hill."

Hinata shut his mouth. His face looked as if somebody had taken something off him, which somebody had.

"Yes, Coach," said Hinata and Kageyama, at exactly the same moment in exactly the same voice, and then glared at each other for having done it.

They lost that 1 too, 20-25. Karasuno went up the hill again. Ukai sat on the bench afterward and found he was tired somewhere behind his eyes, and that he wanted a proper cigarette more than he'd wanted anything all week.

---

That night he stood at the vending machines again, with a cigarette that was lit, and Takeda came and stood beside him with 2 cans of cold tea and gave him 1.

The moon was up over the hill behind the school, small and white. From the 3rd gym at the far edge of the campus came the steady thud of balls on a wooden floor.

"He's in there," said Takeda. "Kiyose-kun. Until 9:20."

"I know."

"Then he goes and phones his sister."

"I know that too."

Takeda drank his tea.

"Can I ask what you'd call it?" he said. "What he does. You said IQ, before exams."

"It's IQ," said Ukai.

He smoked half the cigarette. Takeda waited.

"And an arm," said Ukai.

"An arm?"

"I've been using him as a passer who can serve. Back row, receive, defense. He's good at all of it, so I kept using him for it." He tapped ash into the tin by the machine. "He's a wing spiker, sensei. He's an ace. You should've seen him in hitting lines this morning, when he wasn't being careful. I know what an ace's arm looks like. That's 1."

Takeda was quiet.

"He needs 5 kilos," said Ukai. "Maybe more. Legs, shoulders. A year of somebody putting bad balls up for him. And the weight room, if there is 1."

"I'll find the key."

"And I want him to stop being polite about it." Ukai looked across the dark grass at the lit windows of the 3rd gym. "He'll stand in the back row passing for 3 years if I let him, because he's good at it and it's useful. That's on me."

He finished the cigarette and put it out carefully on the tin.

"My grandfather said a thing," he said. "When I took him over there. *That one sees the hit before the hitter does.* I've been working on it for 3 weeks. I thought he meant the eyes."

"Didn't he?"

"Probably." Ukai pushed his hands into his pockets. "But he's an old coach. 50 years of hitters. He'd have seen the arm too, from the door." He laughed, short. "He'd have seen it before I did. He always does."

Footsteps on the path. They both turned.

It was Nekomata, coming from the staff building in a cardigan with his hands behind his back, small and old and smiling at nothing.

"Ukai-kun. Takeda-sensei. Good evening." He beamed at them. "Your 13. The 1 who goes still and digs Bokuto-kun. I'd like to borrow him tomorrow, after lunch. For half an hour. I have some tape I'd like to show him."

Ukai looked at him.

"His doctor gives him 2 hours of screens a day," he said. "Don't use them all."

"Half an hour," said Nekomata. "I promise." And he nodded to them both, and went on along the path toward the 3rd gym, where the thud of the balls was still coming out into the dark.

Ukai watched him go up to the lit doorway and stand in it, a small dark shape against the light, with his hands behind his back, watching whatever was going on inside. He didn't go in. He just stood there and watched, the way old coaches did.

Ukai's grandfather stood like that.

He could go over there. Tell the old cat to leave his 1st year alone. Tell him he wasn't to turn the kid into an analyst; the kid had a bad enough habit of writing things down already.

He stayed where he was.

He'd tell the kid himself, when they got home, at Karasuno, in a room behind the 1st gym with a squat rack in it and the baseball club's rusty plates, with the key in his hand. *You're a wing spiker. Act like it.* He'd say it properly, the way you said things that mattered.

"Sensei," he said.

"Yes?"

"The key. Don't forget."

"I won't," said Takeda.

**Continuity (ch 38)**

- **Ukai Keishin POV (surprise POV 2 of Arc 3; close third, past tense).** Day 6 of the Shinzen camp, 6 a.m. to night. No status card.
- **Camp calendar (fixed): 8 days counting the arrival morning; last day = day 8** (ch 37's line corrected to "ends in 3 days"). Day 7: ch 39 and ch 40 (3v3 at night). Day 8: ch 41 (Shinzen, the flip), ch 42 (Nekoma), ch 43 to 44 (Ubugawa brief, Fukurodani final).
- **Ukai's soul beats:** can't sleep on futons (bed above the shop, trucks at 4); unlit cigarette between 2 fingers; **26, coaching since spring, "a man... who ran a convenience store, standing in other people's gyms in a tracksuit, pretending"**; "*Old Ukai's grandson*"; the kid saw Nekoma's back row shift in May before he did; pride annoyed for a week or so, then he read the notebook. **Keishin was Karasuno's setter under his grandfather (canon setter; "3 years" invented).** **The hand-up rule is his own:** made in June under the awning by the drainpipe after Seijoh, out of his guilt at 21 all ("I left you on... because we were winning and I wanted it"); an old teammate played 2 sets on a broken ankle under Ikkei (invented).
- **6 a.m.:** watched Sho pass Yaku's 10 through the window. Nekomata told Takeda on day 2: "*Your tall 1 turned up at 6 and begged Yaku to bully him.*"
- **Breakfast: Hinata and Kageyama shouting again, the 1st time in 3 weeks (canon ch 90, back in action; wording invented).**
- **Hitting lines (the ace seed, from Ukai's eyes):** approach a hair slow on the last 2 steps; clean plant, left knee straight over the toe; both arms up, the bow, hips-trunk-shoulder-arm, contact at the top in front of the right shoulder; cross kill "like a door slamming in an empty house"; 1 into the net (ran under it). **About 70% all week; Ukai had been "too busy looking at what the kid could see to look at what he could do."** Never asked his middle-school position. "Asahi was graduating in March." **"Is there a weight room at Karasuno?" "There's a room with some weights in it. Behind the 1st gym. The baseball club used it." "Find out. When we get home."**
- **Game 1, day 6 vs Ubugawa: lost 23-25.** Kiyose on the wing in Tanaka's spot, whole set. Tanaka: 2 seconds of quiet, the grin, the slap: "Don't be bad." "I'll try not to, senpai." "If you're bad I'm going in." Rule: "Hand up, you come off."
- **What Ukai saw:** Ubugawa hid #9 near the net. **Sho served their ace #1 (deep on the left of the passing line) to make him pass; late out to the antenna; setter went middle; Tsukishima blocked.** Ukai explains: "If the ace passes, he's late to hit, or he's tired when he hits... your 1st serve at a team you don't know, you go at the guy who's going to hit next." "I didn't teach him that." **Back row: off the outside edge of Tsukishima's block; 2 balls off Tsukishima's hands came to him. Front row vs Ubugawa's right side: early once (hit over), then waited a beat and a half; soft block up to Noya ("NICE TOUCH!").** **11-13: "MINE!", set high outside right to Asahi (smallest block); 12-13.** "I didn't know he could set." "Neither did I." **15-18: Noya's blind save to the left antenna; full swing into a triple block, stuffed (15-19); looked at the blockers; 3 rallies later, a good high ball: tooled off the end blocker's little finger.** Tanaka: "THAT'S MY SPOT YOU'RE BEING GOOD IN!" Ukai remembers earlier tools (Date Tech after Aone's stuffs; Oikawa at 24-23). (The out-of-system KILL stays for ch 43-44.)
- **21-22:** the stop (heels down), 2 steps before #1 hit, perfect dig; long rally lost, 21-23. **Sho's right hand came up halfway and hung.** Tanaka already at the sub zone with the paddle ("I'll be watching for it", June). **"Halfway wasn't the rule. The rule was shoulder height."** Ukai at the line, low so only Sho heard: **"Oi. Kiyose... Burning?"** Sho checked honestly (counting the till). **"...No." (surprised at his own hand) "Then stay on."** Tanaka sat back down. Ukai's hand not steady with the cigarette.
- End: 21-23 to 23-24 (Tsukishima block; Asahi swing off Sho's pass), last serve at Sho, good pass, Hinata (eyes open) into the block: 23-25. Up the hill.
- **Game 2 vs Shinzen: at 8-12, Ukai's timeout bans the new quick (canon decision; wording invented):** "It's not ready... In practice, every night... In a game, no. Not until it works in practice 7 times out of 10... normal quicks. 1st tempo... You lose a game here, you run a hill. You lose a game at the Spring High, you go home. I'd rather you ran the hill." "Yes, Coach" together, glaring. **Lost 20-25** (Karasuno still winless at camp; the 1st win is ch 41).
- **Night, vending machines, Takeda:** "It's IQ." ... **"And an arm... He's a wing spiker, sensei. He's an ace... He needs 5 kilos... Legs, shoulders. A year of somebody putting bad balls up for him. And the weight room."** "I'll find the key." "That's on me." Ikkei's line ("*That one sees the hit before the hitter does*"), worked on for 3 weeks: "He'd have seen the arm too, from the door."
- **Nekomata (cardigan):** "I'd like to borrow him tomorrow, after lunch. For half an hour. I have some tape." Ukai: "His doctor gives him 2 hours of screens a day. Don't use them all." "Half an hour. I promise." Nekomata stood in the 3rd gym doorway watching, like Ikkei. Ukai stayed put. **He'll tell Sho at home, in the weight room behind the 1st gym, with the key: "*You're a wing spiker. Act like it.*"** "The key. Don't forget." "I won't."
- Next: ch 39 "All of it" (Sho): day 7: Nekomata's tape after lunch ("You'd make a fine analyst." "I'd rather play."); Akaashi teaches him hands (Set E to D, status card); the 9:30 call where he tells Mio all of it (including the hand that went halfway up on its own, "No", staying on; playing the wing). The weight-room news is Ukai's to deliver at home (not in ch 39).

## Chapter 39: All of it

At lunch on the 7th day Ukai stopped beside Sho's chair with his tray.

"Nekomata-sensei wants you," he said. "Half an hour, after you've eaten. The staff room. He's got tape." He looked at Sho for a beat. "Half an hour. Then you come back to me."

"Yes, Coach."

Ukai went on to his seat. Tanaka, opposite, leaned over his rice and said in a low thrilled voice, "Nekoma's coach? What did you DO?" and Sho said he didn't know, which was true.

---

The staff room had air conditioning.

It was a small room on the ground floor of Shinzen's main building, with a long table covered in clipboards and score sheets and empty tea cans, a whiteboard with the week's games on it in 3 colors of marker, and, on a trolley in the corner, a television the size of a microwave with a camcorder plugged into the front of it by a tangle of cable. The air conditioner hummed. After 7 days of gyms it felt like walking into a fridge.

Kenma was on the floor in the corner, with his back against the wall and his console on his knees.

He looked up when Sho came in behind Nekomata. His eyes went to Sho's face and then down to his feet. Then back to the console.

"Kozume likes it in here," said Nekomata, pulling out 2 chairs in front of the television. "It's cool and nobody shouts. Sit."

Sho sat. Nekomata sat beside him with the remote, a small old man in a cardigan who smelled of mint sweets and the gym, and pressed play.

---

It was Karasuno against Ubugawa, yesterday's 1st game, filmed high and from the side. 1 of Nekoma's 1st years had been up in the gallery with the camera; Sho remembered seeing him there, a small boy with his elbows on the rail.

"Their #1," said Nekomata. "Watch him."

A plain float coming at #1's chest. Sho watched himself, a small figure in black at the end line, hit it. #1 passed it, and then had to turn and run out to the antenna, late, and Ubugawa's setter looked at him and set the middle instead.

Nekomata paused it.

"Why him?"

"So he has to pass," said Sho. "Then he's late to hit."

"Hm." Play. "And this."

The soft block, from the side. Sho watched himself wait at the net, a beat and a half, his hands up and his knees bent, and then go up late with his hands angled back, and the ball go up off them into his own court, to Nishinoya.

"Who taught you to wait?"

"Nobody yet, sir. I'd jumped too early the time before."

Nekomata laughed, small and dry and pleased, and let it run on: the 2nd-ball set, the bad ball at the antenna and the triple block, the ball coming down off their hands like a stone. 3 rallies later, the ball off the end blocker's little finger and away into the stands.

He paused it there.

"I watched you swing at that triple block," he said. "Full swing. I thought, that boy is either stupid or an ace." He looked sideways at Sho. "Then I watched this 1."

He didn't say which he'd decided. He took the tape out and put another in.

---

This 1 Sho hadn't played in. Fukurodani against Shinzen, from the 3rd day, the same high angle.

Nekomata played a few seconds, a serve going up, a pass coming off somebody's arms, and paused it with the ball in the air.

"Well?"

"Middle," said Sho. "Akaashi-san's hips are square and their middle's already in the air."

Play. Middle.

"Well?"

"Shinzen's #4 on the right. He's been sulking since he was blocked. Their setter's going to give him an easy 1 to bring him back."

Play. #4, an easy high ball, which he hit hard into the floor and then punched the air.

"Well?"

"Tip. Over the block. Bokuto-san's elbow is low."

Tip.

"Well?"

"Line. Back corner. He's hit cross 6 times, their libero's cheating cross, and Akaashi-san knows it."

Line.

It went on. Nekomata paused, and Sho said, and Nekomata played, and it was right, and right, and once wrong, and right. Somewhere around the 8th Sho noticed that he was sitting forward on the chair with his elbows on his knees and his chin out toward the little screen, and that the room had gone quiet apart from the air conditioner, and that he was enjoying himself. Enjoying it the way he'd enjoyed it for 9 years, alone, at night, with a cold coffee going colder beside the keyboard.

His thumb was moving against his knee. A small movement, back and forth. He looked down at it.

It was the movement his thumb made on a rewind button.

He sat back in the chair, and put both hands flat on his thighs.

Nekomata had paused the tape again with the ball in the air. He didn't say "Well?" this time. He was looking at Sho, sideways, with his small bright old eyes.

"You'd make a fine analyst," said Nekomata.

The air conditioner hummed. In the corner, Kenma's console made a small chiming sound and stopped, as if he'd paused it too.

"Thank you, sir," said Sho. "I'd rather play."

Nekomata looked at him a little longer. Then he smiled, all his wrinkles at once, and pressed the button, and the screen went blue.

"Good," he said. "So would I, at your age." He started winding the cable round his hand. "In Tokyo I said lookers get tired. Watching takes it out of you." He glanced at Sho's face, at his eyes, the way a doctor glances. "You don't look tired."

"I'm not, sir."

"Hm," said Nekomata, and put the camcorder in its case. "Half an hour. I promised your coach. Off you go."

Sho stood up and bowed. At the door he heard Kenma's voice from the corner, flat, with his eyes still on the console.

"Your stop's different."

Sho turned round.

"It used to be a dot," said Kenma. "Now it's a circle." He pressed a button. "It's annoying."

"Thank you," said Sho.

"You say that a lot," said Kenma, to the console, and Sho went out into the heat.

---

Karasuno played 4 games that afternoon and lost all 4, but 2 of them by only 2 points, and on the hill afterward Tanaka yelled the whole way up for the 1st time since the 1st day.

Hinata and Kageyama ran the old quick in games, the way Coach had said, and it went in, a lot. Every time it did, Hinata's face did something complicated, pleased and unhappy at once. In the evening, before dinner, the 2 of them went off to a corner of the main gym to run the new 1, eyes open, and it went into the net, and they shouted at each other, and ran it again.

Sho played the wing for a whole set against Nekoma.

It was the 2nd whole set of his life on the wing, and it went the way a 2nd anything goes, worse than the 1st and better. He hit 9 balls. 3 of them went in, 2 were dug by Yaku as if Yaku had been told about them in advance, 2 were blocked, and 2 went out because he swung harder than the set deserved. He served Kuroo, to make Kuroo pass, and Kuroo passed it and grinned at him through the net and called him "Notebook" in a voice that meant he'd liked it. Kenma never sent a ball into Sho's corner on purpose, and never sent it anywhere Sho had crossed out, either.

In the 3rd game, against Fukurodani, Kageyama dug a ball off Bokuto's hand flat out on the floor, and Karasuno had no setter again, and Sho said "MINE!" and got under it and set Asahi the same high ball outside he'd set yesterday.

It went too tight. Asahi had to reach for it, and Fukurodani's block was waiting, and it came straight back down.

Sho looked at his own hands. They were a passer's hands, flat and stiff. They'd pushed the ball roughly the right way, not quite high enough and a bit too close to the net.

On the other side of the net, Akaashi was looking at him.

---

He asked at 6:15, after the last hill, when everybody else was going to the baths.

Akaashi was in the corner of the main gym, alone, picking up stray balls and putting them in the basket, as he did every evening. Sho came across the empty court and picked up the ball nearest his feet and put it in the basket too.

"Akaashi-san. Could you teach me hands? Setting hands. 20 minutes, before the 3rd gym." He bowed. "I'll pay."

Akaashi straightened up with a ball in each hand. "Pay in what?"

"Bokuto-san."

Akaashi's face went still, the way it went still at the top of a set.

"Go on," he said.

"When he's about to go quiet, his approach gets shorter. 1 step. A couple of rallies before."

"I know that 1."

"He touches his hair with his left hand after a hit he doesn't like. If it's his right hand, he's fine."

Akaashi nodded, once.

"And he stops saying your name," said Sho. "When he's happy, he calls *Akaashi!* for the set, every time. The rally before he goes quiet, he still calls for it, but he says *Here!* Or *Hey!* I counted. Against Ubugawa yesterday. On the 2nd night in the 3rd gym. On the 1st day, against us. Every time."

The gym was very quiet. Outside, Tanaka was yelling about the bath.

Akaashi stood by the basket with his hands at his sides, and his eyes went inward, as if he were running back through a great many rallies at once.

"I didn't know that 1," he said at last.

"Then I've paid."

Akaashi picked up a ball from the basket and turned it over in his hands, as he had in the 3rd gym before he'd set Bokuto back to himself.

"Show me your hands," he said.

---

Sho put them up.

"Too flat," said Akaashi. "And too late. You're making the shape when the ball arrives. Make it before." He came and stood beside Sho and put his own hands up above his forehead, fingers spread, thumbs and forefingers making a small window between them. "Like this. Before it comes. So the ball comes into a shape that's already there, and your hands close round it."

Sho made the window.

"The ball touches here." Akaashi touched the pads of his own fingers, the top 2 joints. "The palm's for hitting. These are for setting. Thumbs under it."

He tossed a ball over Sho's head.

Sho set it. It went up, and forward, and came down a meter short of where he'd aimed.

"Your arms did all of it," said Akaashi. "Your arms are tired, you've been passing and hitting all day. Use your legs. Bend, and the ball comes down into your hands, and then you stand up, and the ball goes up because you stood up." He did it slowly, without a ball: knees, hips, then the trunk, arms last, then the wrists, a long smooth push from the floor to his fingertips. "It's like hitting. Upside down. Legs 1st, then the trunk, the arms last. The hands only finish it." He tilted his head, very slightly. "You know how to hit."

Sho stood still.

"I watched yesterday," said Akaashi. He tossed another ball. "Again."

They did 20 sets. Then 20 more. Akaashi tossed and watched and said very little. "Earlier." "Legs." "Your left hand's lazy." "Better." He'd hung a towel on the antenna at the far end of the net, high, at the place where Bokuto liked to hit, and every set went to the towel.

On the 1st 20, 6 came down near enough that a hitter could have hit them.

On the 2nd 20, 11.

On the 3rd 20, he counted for himself, and it was 14. Akaashi made him do 1 more, because the 20th toss had been a bad 1, and the 21st came down beside the towel. 15.

Akaashi caught the last ball as it fell and held it.

"That's enough for 1 day," he said. "Your fingers will hurt tomorrow. That's normal." He put the ball in the basket. "Your back set will be bad for a year. Your front set is ordinary now. For a hitter's 2nd ball, ordinary is enough. Nobody expects anything from a hitter's 2nd ball."

"Thank you, Akaashi-san."

Akaashi took the towel down off the antenna and folded it in half and then in half again.

"*Here,*" he said, quietly, to the towel. "Instead of *Akaashi.*" Then, to Sho: "How long have you been listening to hitters?"

"A long time," said Sho.

Akaashi looked at him, a long plain look. Then he nodded, and went to find Bokuto.

---

He sat on the bench outside the dining hall at 9:30, under the light with the moths, and called home.

"Who'd you play," said Mio.

He told her. 4 games, 4 lost, 2 of them by 2. 4 times up the hill, and Tanaka-senpai yelling the whole way, which meant he was happy. Then the old coach from Nekoma, and the staff room, and the tape.

"He paused it and I said what would happen next. And it did. And he said I'd make a good analyst."

"What's an analyst?"

"Somebody who watches other people play and writes it down."

Mio thought about it.

"You already do that."

"Yes."

"Is it bad?"

"It's a good job. You can do it till you're 80." He looked across the dark grass at the lit windows of the 3rd gym. "I said I'd rather play."

"Good," said Mio. "And?"

"And Akaashi-san taught me to set. With my fingers. My fingers hurt."

"Is that good?"

"He said I'm ordinary now. For me, that's good."

Scratch. "Okay. Eyes."

"Not today."

"Okay." Scratch. "Is that all?"

He sat with the phone against his ear.

He'd decided on the hill, on the 3rd time up, with his legs full of sand.

"No," said Sho. "Yesterday something happened that I didn't tell you."

The line went quiet.

"What?"

"In the 1st game, Coach put me on the wing. The whole set. Hitting. And today again, against Nekoma."

"Is that good?"

"It's where I want to be," said Sho. "It's where I'm supposed to be. I've been passing all summer because I'm good at passing and the team needs it."

"That's not a reason," said Mio at once.

"Why not?"

"You're good at maths. You don't do maths club." A pause. "Did you hit it?"

"Yesterday I hit it into 3 people and it came straight back down on my head. And then 3 rallies later I hit it off the edge of 1 of them and it went into the crowd. Today I hit 9. 3 went in."

"So that's bad."

"It's still bad. It's where I want to be."

Scratch. "What else."

He looked at the moths.

"In the same game yesterday," he said, "near the end. The eye came. I told you that. It didn't hurt. I told you that too. But after the rally, my hand went up. By itself. Halfway. Like I was going to put it up for Coach, the way I'm supposed to if it burns. But it didn't burn. My hand just went."

He heard her breathing.

"You told me the eye," said Mio slowly. "Yesterday. You didn't tell me the hand."

"No."

"That was yesterday."

"Yes."

"You said all of it." Her voice had gone very small and very fierce. "You promised. After counts, if you tell me after. All of it. Or I win."

"I know," said Sho. "You win. 5 puddings."

There was a long silence on the line. He could hear the television in the living room at home, low, and Mom saying something to Dad.

"Did you come off?" said Mio at last. "When your hand went up."

"Coach came to the line. He asked if it was burning. I checked. Properly. Like you check your pocket for your keys. And it wasn't. So I said no. And he said, *Then stay on.*"

Another silence. Then, very carefully, the way she said things she was working out as she went:

"So your hand's scared."

He opened his mouth. Closed it.

"Your eyes are fine," said Mio. "The doctor said 120. It didn't hurt. So it's your hand. Your hand's scared it'll burn again, so it goes up in case."

He sat on the bench with the phone against his ear and the moon going white over the hill, and thought about a gym in Osaka, and floor wax, and a setter calling his name.

"Maybe," he said.

"Then tell it it's fine," said Mio. "When it does it. Say, *it's fine, you can go down.*" A pause. "And if it's not fine, put it all the way up."

"Okay."

"Promise."

"I promise."

Scratch. Scratch. A long scratch.

"What are you writing?"

"8," said Mio. "*Hand goes up when he's scared.* And I put *(GOOD)* after it. In brackets."

He laughed. It came out of him all at once on the bench under the light, and a moth flew into his face, and he laughed harder.

"What?" said Mio, offended. "It IS good."

"I know. I know it is."

Scratch.

"4 puddings," said Mio.

"You said 5."

"4. Because you told me today. Today counts a bit." A pause. "Is that all of it?"

He sat there and checked, properly, the way he'd checked for Coach.

"That's all of it," said Sho.

---

He was still on the bench when Hinata came past at a run in his practice clothes, his hair wet from the bath and sticking up in every direction, heading across the dark grass toward the 3rd gym.

He saw Sho and skidded to a stop.

"Kiyose! I asked Bokuto-san! At dinner! I asked him to teach me how to fight in the air, and he said YES!" He was bouncing on his toes. "You go every night, right? Come on! Come and watch at least!"

"I've just come out."

"Come back in!" Hinata was already running backward across the grass. "He said 3 on 3! He said it's going to be cool!"

The door of the 3rd gym banged open, and Bokuto's voice came out into the dark like a foghorn.

"HEY HEY HEY! KARASUNO'S 10! YOU CAME!"

Hinata turned and ran.

Sho stayed on the bench, looking at the lit door. His fingertips ached.

Then he got up, and went to watch.

```
[Kiyose Sho]
Age: 15 | Height: 182cm | Karasuno #13
Power: C   Jump: B   Speed: C   Stamina: B
Reception: C   Serve: D   Block: E   Set: D (from E)
Mentality: B   Shamelessness: S
Emperor's Eye: Partial awakening
Meta Vision: Scanning
```

**Continuity (ch 39)**

- Sho POV, day 7 of the 8-day Shinzen camp. **Status card: Set E to D** (Akaashi's sets to the towel: 6/20, 11/20, 14/20 plus a redo = 15; "Your front set is ordinary now"). No eye that day.
- **Lunch:** Ukai: "Nekomata-sensei wants you. Half an hour... He's got tape... Then you come back to me." Tanaka: "Nekoma's coach? What did you DO?"
- **Staff room (air conditioning; TV on a trolley; camcorder; Kenma in the corner):** Tape 1: Karasuno vs Ubugawa, day 6, filmed from the gallery by a Nekoma 1st year (Nekoma has no manager). "Why him?" "So he has to pass. Then he's late to hit." The soft block ("Who taught you to wait?" "Nobody yet, sir. I'd jumped too early the time before."). The 2nd-ball set; the triple block; the tool. **Nekomata: "I watched you swing at that triple block... that boy is either stupid or an ace... Then I watched this 1."** (undecided aloud). Tape 2: **Fukurodani vs Shinzen, day 3 (a game Sho didn't play):** "Well?" calls with the ball paused: middle (Akaashi's hips), Shinzen #4 sulking gets an easy 1, Bokuto tip (low elbow), line (libero cheating cross); right, right, once wrong, right. **Sho's thumb made the rewind motion; he sat back, hands flat.** **"You'd make a fine analyst." "Thank you, sir. I'd rather play."** "Good. So would I, at your age." **"In Tokyo I said lookers get tired... You don't look tired." "I'm not, sir." "Hm."** **Kenma: "Your stop's different... It used to be a dot. Now it's a circle. It's annoying." "Thank you." "You say that a lot."**
- **Day 7 games:** 4 lost, 2 by 2 points; Tanaka yelled up the hill. Hinata and Kageyama ran the old quick in games (worked) and the new 1 in a corner before dinner (theirs). **Sho on the wing a whole set vs Nekoma (his 2nd on the wing): 9 swings, 3 kills, 2 dug by Yaku, 2 blocked, 2 out (swung harder than the set deserved); served Kuroo to make him pass ("Notebook").** Kenma never sent a ball into his corner on purpose or anywhere he'd ruled out. **Vs Fukurodani: 2nd-ball set to Asahi too tight; stuffed.** Akaashi watched.
- **Akaashi, 6:15 (picks up balls every evening):** "Could you teach me hands?... I'll pay." "Pay in what?" "Bokuto-san." Tells: shorter approach ("I know that 1"); left hand to the hair (a nod); **"*Here!*" instead of "*Akaashi!*" the rally before he goes quiet (vs Ubugawa day 6; 2nd night in the 3rd gym; day 1 vs Karasuno). "I didn't know that 1." "Then I've paid."**
- **Akaashi's hands:** make the shape before the ball arrives (small window between thumbs and forefingers; hands close round the ball); pads of the top 2 joints ("The palm's for hitting. These are for setting."); thumbs under; legs first ("It's like hitting. Upside down... You know how to hit." "I watched yesterday."); "Earlier." "Legs." "Your left hand's lazy." Towel on the far antenna where Bokuto hits. "Your fingers will hurt tomorrow... Your back set will be bad for a year. Your front set is ordinary now... Nobody expects anything from a hitter's 2nd ball." **"*Here.* Instead of *Akaashi.* How long have you been listening to hitters?" "A long time."**
- **9:30 call, "All of it":** scores; Nekomata ("You already do that." "Is it bad?" "It's a good job. You can do it till you're 80... I said I'd rather play." "Good."); Akaashi ("ordinary"). Eyes: "Not today." **"Yesterday something happened that I didn't tell you":** the wing ("It's where I want to be. It's where I'm supposed to be... passing all summer because I'm good at passing" / Mio: "That's not a reason... You're good at maths. You don't do maths club."); stuffed then tooled; today 9 swings, 3 in. **The hand halfway up: "You told me the eye. Yesterday. You didn't tell me the hand... You promised... Or I win." "I know. You win. 5 puddings."** (ch 26 terms.) "*Then stay on.*" **Mio: "So your hand's scared... Then tell it it's fine... And if it's not fine, put it all the way up." "Promise." "I promise."** Osaka image (gym, floor wax, a setter calling his name). **Notebook: *8. Hand goes up when he's scared. (GOOD)*** Sho laughed (moth in the face). **"4 puddings... Because you told me today. Today counts a bit."** "That's all of it."
- **Hook:** Hinata (asked Bokuto himself at dinner; canon ch 91: Hinata goes to Bokuto; Bokuto's lessons to Hinata are Hinata's): "I asked him to teach me how to fight in the air, and he said YES!... 3 on 3!" Bokuto: "HEY HEY HEY! KARASUNO'S 10! YOU CAME!" Sho's fingertips ached; he went to watch.
- Ace path: Nekomata's "stupid or an ace"; Akaashi's "You know how to hit"; 2nd whole set on the wing (3/9); Sho to Mio: "It's where I'm supposed to be." Ukai hasn't told him yet (weight room at home).
- Next: ch 40 "Owls and cats" (Sho): night 7, the 3rd gym; the canon 3v3 (canon lineups; Bokuto's feint and "use the block" lessons to Hinata are Hinata's); Sho watches; then (invented) a 2nd 3v3 where he plays on the map only ("3 people. I can hold 3.") and it's fun; late on things and laughs. Hinata: "What's yours?"

## Chapter 40: Owls and cats

There were 6 of them on the court when Sho came in at 9:40, and the court had been cut in half.

Somebody had dragged the net standards in, so the net ran across only half the old gym's floor, and the other half was empty boards with the moths' shadows going round on them. On 1 side of the half-net stood Bokuto, Akaashi and Hinata. On the other, Kuroo, Lev and Tsukishima.

Tsukishima stood at the net beside Kuroo with his glasses pushed up and his hands loose at his sides, as if he'd been coming here every night of his life.

"13!" Bokuto saw Sho in the doorway and threw both arms in the air. "You came back! You're the audience! Every great match needs an audience! Sit there!" He pointed at the stage. Then he slapped his own chest, and Akaashi's shoulder, and the top of Hinata's head. "OWLS," he announced, "versus CATS!"

"Why are we cats?" said Lev.

"Because you're Nekoma," said Kuroo. "Keep up."

"Then why is Tsukishima a cat? He's Karasuno."

"Tonight he's a cat." Kuroo looked sideways. "Aren't you, Tsukki?"

"Don't call me that," said Tsukishima, to the net.

Sho went and sat on the edge of the stage with his legs hanging over, beside the dusty red curtain with the dent in it. He'd left the notebook in the classroom, in his bag, under his towel. His fingertips ached from Akaashi's sets.

---

He'd played a game like this once, 3 against 3, on a Saturday in April in the Second Gymnasium at Karasuno. Hinata and Kageyama and Tanaka on 1 side. On the other, Tsukishima, Yamaguchi, and him. It was the day the eye had opened for the 1st time, a blink early on Hinata's 1st real quick, and he'd dug a ball nobody should have dug and stood there afterward on the floor without the faintest idea what had happened.

Tonight Tsukishima was on the other side of the net from Hinata again, and Sho was on the stage.

Akaashi set Hinata.

It was a high set, the kind that hangs at the top and waits for you, and Hinata ran in under it from the left and went up, and up, his eyes wide open. Sho could see them from the stage. He could see Hinata looking: at the net, at the 3 pairs of hands coming up across it, Kuroo's long careful ones, Lev's enormous wild ones, Tsukishima's, quieter than both. He could see Hinata seeing them. It was the thing Hinata had shouted at Kageyama in the dark outside the gym 2 weeks ago, *I want to see what's in front of me*, and here it was, in front of him, 3 people high, and he could see all of it.

He hit it as hard as he could, into the middle of them.

It came back off 6 hands with a sound like a dropped book and hit the floor at his feet. Hinata landed on top of it, and rolled, and lay on his back on the boards with his arms out.

Akaashi's face didn't change. He walked over and picked up the ball and turned it in his hands and looked down at Hinata on the floor.

"Again?" said Akaashi.

"AGAIN!" said Hinata, to the ceiling, and was up.

Akaashi set him again. The wall went up. Hinata hit into it. The ball came down at his feet. Lev shouted with delight and Kuroo said "Nice, Lev," and Tsukishima came down off the block with his face shut, and Hinata picked himself up and walked back to his spot, breathing hard, with his jaw set.

The 3rd time, before Akaashi could toss, Bokuto came over and crouched beside Hinata and said something, low, with his hands moving. Sho was too far away to hear it. 1 of Bokuto's hands made a fist, and then opened, and pushed forward, slowly, soft.

Sho looked away, at the moths going round the nearest light, and kept his eyes there until he heard Akaashi's hands on the ball.

---

Between rallies, on the other side of the net, Kuroo was talking to Tsukishima.

Sho could see that much. Kuroo leaning in with his head down; Tsukishima beside him with his arms folded, looking straight ahead at the tape, apparently not listening at all. Kuroo held up a flat hand and moved it, and said something, and Tsukishima's face stayed exactly where it was.

On the next rally, when Bokuto came in from the left, Tsukishima waited.

He waited longer than he had all night. Then he went up, late and soft, with his hands angled back, and Bokuto's ball came off them and went up, high and slow, into the Cats' own court, and Kuroo, behind him, dug it, laughing.

"HEY!" said Bokuto, delighted and outraged at once. "HEY! He WAITED!"

Tsukishima came down off the block and pushed his glasses up.

Sho realized he'd been holding his breath, and let it go.

He sat on the edge of the stage with his hands on his knees and watched. A rally. Another. Hinata stuffed again, and up again. Bokuto shouting at Lev to jump higher, which made Lev jump later. Kuroo laughing on his back on the floor after a dig. The smell of old wood and cut grass coming in through the windows that didn't shut.

In the room with the monitors a good play had been a clip, something to cut and label and send to a coach by 8 in the morning. He watched Tsukishima wait at the net again, a beat and then another beat, and go up late, and the ball come off his hands to Kuroo, and he didn't reach for a pen he didn't have.

---

At about 9:55 Hinata came and threw himself down on the stage beside Sho, flat on his back, soaked, chest going like a bellows.

"Water," said Hinata, to the ceiling.

Sho passed him his bottle. Hinata sat up and drank half of it in 1 go and wiped his mouth on his arm.

"3 blockers," he said. "Did you see? 3! And I was looking at them the whole time. With my eyes open." He was grinning so wide it looked like it hurt. "They blocked me every time. But I *saw* them do it."

He turned his head and looked at Sho, still breathing hard, his hair stuck to his forehead in points.

"Bokuto-san says everybody needs a weapon. A thing that's yours. His is everything, basically. He's like that." He held his own hand up in front of his face and looked at it, and then closed it, slowly, and opened it again, the way Bokuto's had. "I'm getting mine tonight."

He let the hand drop onto his chest.

"What's yours?"

Sho looked at the court.

Akaashi was picking up balls. Bokuto was trying to teach Lev how to jump, standing beside him and jumping too, very high, over and over, which was making Lev worse. Kuroo was sitting on the floor with his back against the net post, laughing at them. Tsukishima was at the net alone, looking at his own hands.

What was his.

He couldn't say the eye. He'd never said it to anybody. Even Mio, who knew it was there and kept its score, only called it *eyes*.

"I'm good at receive," he started, and stopped, because it was true and it wasn't the answer, and Hinata was looking at him the way he looked at a ball he was about to hit.

Sho turned the bottle over in his hands. A moth landed on the back of his wrist and sat there, opening and closing its wings, and flew off again.

"The bad ball," he said.

Hinata blinked. "Huh?"

"When it's all gone wrong. No setter. Somebody has to hit it."

Hinata stared at him.

He stared for so long that the moths went round the light twice. Then his eyes went wide, slowly, all the way, the way they went wide at the top of a jump.

"That's the ace's ball," said Hinata.

"Yes."

"I want that ball too!" Hinata sat bolt upright on the stage. "I'm going to be the ace! I'm going to be... we can't *both* have it!"

Sho didn't answer that.

"HINATA!" Bokuto roared from the court. "OWLS! BACK! NOW!"

Hinata bounced off the stage as if it had thrown him and ran. Halfway across the court he turned round and pointed at Sho, running backward, and opened his mouth to say something, and then didn't, and just pointed, fiercely, and turned back.

---

It happened 4 rallies later, with Sho on the stage.

Akaashi set Hinata high on the left, a set that waited. Hinata came in and went up with his eyes open, and across the net Tsukishima waited, the way Kuroo had told him, a beat longer, and went up late with his hands angled, ready to soften the hard ball and send it back up to Kuroo.

Hinata's whole arm went back, full, hard.

At the top, his hand opened.

It came forward slowly and soft and pushed the ball up over Tsukishima's late fingertips, and the ball dropped just behind them, gently, onto the 1 piece of the Cats' court where nobody was standing, between Tsukishima's back and Kuroo's front. Kuroo dived. He was a long way too late.

The ball rolled to a stop by the net post.

Hinata landed and stared at it.

"I..." He looked at his own hand. He looked at Bokuto. "I DID IT! BOKUTO-SAN! I DID IT!"

"YOU DID IT!" Bokuto picked Hinata up round the middle and swung him in a circle. "THAT'S IT! THAT'S THE ONE!"

Tsukishima stood at the net and looked at the ball by the post, and then at Hinata, being swung round, and pushed his glasses up, and his mouth did something very small at 1 corner, and then stopped.

On the stage, Sho sat with his hands on his knees and found he was grinning so hard his face ached.

---

10 minutes later Tsukishima came down from a block and said, to nobody in particular, "I'm done for tonight," and walked off the court to his bag.

"NO!" said Bokuto. "We're not done! It's 2 games to 2! We need a decider!" He looked round the gym wildly, at the empty half of the floor, at the door, at the stage.

His eyes landed on Sho.

"13! You're a cat now!"

Sho looked at Tsukishima, who was putting on his tracksuit top beside the stage.

"Go on," said Tsukishima, zipping it up. "He won't shut up otherwise."

Sho got down off the stage and walked onto the court and stood at the net in Tsukishima's place, on Tsukishima's side, across from Hinata.

---

3 against 3. He looked across the net at them once, before the 1st serve, and found that once was enough.

He'd dug Bokuto for 5 nights. He'd watched Akaashi set all week and paid him in Bokuto that evening. He'd known Hinata since the 1st week of school. 3 people. He could hold 3.

At Seijoh, in June, he'd tried to hold all 12, both sides, every hand, every foot, and his eyes had stopped working together. In Tokyo he'd held 6 in 1 look, just. Here, there were 3, and he knew all 3, and the whole of the far court sat in his head without his reaching for it, the way your own room sits in your head in the dark.

He left the eye alone.

Akaashi served. Kuroo passed, Lev set it, high and drifting and much too far off the net, and Kuroo went up for it on the right and hit it into Bokuto's hands, and Bokuto roared. 0-1.

Lev served into the net. 0-2.

Akaashi served at Sho, a float that dipped at the end, and Sho saw the toss and the shoulder and went while he was still looking, feet 1st, and stopped where it was going to land, and looked at it again, all the way onto his arms. It went up, soft, to Lev. Lev set it to Sho at the left.

It was too tight to the net. Bokuto was there. Sho went up into it with his whole arm, and at the last instant took some of the arm off it, and rolled it over Bokuto's hands toward the back corner, deep, where nobody was.

Akaashi was there. Of course Akaashi was there; he'd seen Sho take the arm off, from all the way back. He dug it up, and Hinata ran under it and pushed it back over, and Kuroo dug that, and the rally went on, and on, the ball going over the net 6 times, until Bokuto finally hit 1 so hard it went through Lev's hands and into the curtain.

0-3. Sho stood at the net breathing and looked at the curtain, which had 2 dents in it now.

He was late on Hinata.

He'd known he would be. Hinata was faster than anything he'd stood in front of, faster than Bokuto, faster than his own crossing-out. On a full court, behind a block, with Yaku's feet and Ikkei's way of reading, he could get there. Here, with only 3 on each side, Hinata was off the ground before Sho had finished crossing anything out, and the ball was down before Sho's heels were.

"HA!" said Hinata, landing.

Sho got up off the floor and went back to his spot.

They'd rotated by the next rally; Sho was at the back now, behind Kuroo and Lev. He crossed Hinata out faster. He was still late. The time after that, he gave up crossing Hinata out at all and just stood where Hinata had hit the last 2, and Hinata hit it somewhere else, and Sho sat down on the floor where the ball had landed and laughed until Lev asked if he was all right.

He dug 2 of Bokuto's, standing at the edge of Kuroo's shadow. He blocked nothing all game. He hit 4 balls and 1 of them went in. The Owls won it 15-9, and Bokuto ran a lap of the half court with his arms out like an aeroplane.

Kuroo came over and stood beside Sho at the net post, getting his breath back, and watched Bokuto go round.

"You're different tonight, notebook," said Kuroo.

Sho looked at him. "Am I?"

Kuroo looked down at him with his head on 1 side, and seemed to be trying to work out how. Then he gave up.

"Dunno," he said, and went to drink.

---

Daichi came to the door at 10:15.

He stood in it with his arms folded and the corridor light behind him, the way he stood at the gym door at Karasuno at the end of evening practice, and looked at them all: at Hinata, who'd just been put down by Bokuto and was still vibrating; at Sho, sitting on the floor against the net post; at Tsukishima, who had apparently been leaving for 15 minutes.

"Lights out," said Daichi, "was at 10."

"Captain!" Hinata ran at him. "Captain! I did a feint! A real 1! Over Tsukishima!"

"Tell me tomorrow," said Daichi. "Bed." His eyes went past Hinata to Sho on the floor. "You too, Kiyose."

"Yes, Captain."

They walked back across the dark grass in a bunch, Hinata in the middle, demonstrating the feint with his hand in the air every few steps, pushing an invisible ball over an invisible block. Tsukishima walked at the edge with his hands in his pockets. When Hinata pushed the invisible ball for the 6th time, Tsukishima said, "We saw," in a tired voice, and Hinata did it a 7th time, at him, and Tsukishima's mouth went up at 1 corner again and straight back down.

The moon was high over the hill, small and white.

Tomorrow was the last day. 4 games: Shinzen, then Nekoma, then Ubugawa, and Fukurodani last. Karasuno hadn't won a game all week.

---

In the classroom, on the futons, in the dark, Hinata whispered across the gap.

"Kiyose."

"Mm."

"Tomorrow. We're going to win 1."

Sho lay on his back and looked at the dark ceiling, at the faint gray square the window made on it.

"Shinzen's 1st," he said.

"We've never beaten Shinzen."

"I know."

Hinata was quiet for so long that Sho thought he'd gone to sleep. Then he heard him turn over, and settle, and breathe out, long and slow, the way he did when he'd decided something.

Near the door, in the dark, Tanaka said quite clearly in his sleep, "That's my spot," and then, "...rice," and was quiet.

Sho lay with his aching fingers folded on his chest and listened to the cicadas start up again outside, and didn't count anything at all.

**Continuity (ch 40)**

- Sho POV, night 7 of the 8-day Shinzen camp, 9:40 to after 10:15. **No eye (left alone).** No status card.
- **The canon 3v3 on a half court in the 3rd gym** (net standards dragged in): **Owls: Bokuto, Akaashi, Hinata. Cats: Kuroo, Lev, Tsukishima.** Bokuto: "OWLS versus CATS!" Lev: "Why are we cats?" Kuroo: "Because you're Nekoma. Keep up... Tonight he's a cat. Aren't you, Tsukki?" "Don't call me that." Sho on the stage edge beside the dented curtain; notebook left in his bag.
- **April echo:** the Saturday 3v3 at Karasuno (Hinata, Kageyama, Tanaka vs Tsukishima, Yamaguchi, Sho), where the eye 1st opened on Hinata's 1st quick.
- **Hinata's (canon, his):** eyes open, sees the 3 blockers ("*I want to see what's in front of me*", the fight 2 weeks ago); stuffed ("a sound like a dropped book"); Akaashi: "Again?" "AGAIN!" Bokuto crouched and told him something (fist opening, pushing forward soft); Sho looked away at the moths.
- **Tsukishima's (canon, his):** Kuroo talking to him between rallies (flat hand); next rally Tsukishima waited and soft-blocked Bokuto up to Kuroo. Bokuto: "HEY! He WAITED!" Sho held his breath. The monitors image: a good play was a clip; tonight "he didn't reach for a pen he didn't have."
- **"What's yours?" (about 9:55):** Hinata on the stage with Sho's bottle: "They blocked me every time. But I *saw* them do it... Bokuto-san says everybody needs a weapon... His is everything, basically... I'm getting mine tonight." Sho can't say the eye; false start "I'm good at receive"; a moth on his wrist. **"The bad ball." "Huh?" "When it's all gone wrong. No setter. Somebody has to hit it." "That's the ace's ball." "Yes." "I want that ball too!... I'm going to be the ace!... we can't *both* have it!"** Sho didn't answer. Bokuto: "OWLS! BACK! NOW!" Hinata pointed at Sho, running backward.
- **Hinata's feint (in the canon 3v3, Hinata's moment): over Tsukishima's new late block into the empty place between Tsukishima and Kuroo; Kuroo dived too late.** "I DID IT! BOKUTO-SAN!" "THAT'S THE ONE!" Tsukishima's mouth corner. Sho grinning on the stage.
- **The decider (invented):** Tsukishima: "I'm done for tonight." Bokuto (2 games to 2): "13! You're a cat now!" Tsukishima: "Go on. He won't shut up otherwise." **Sho takes Tsukishima's place, across from Hinata.** "3 people. He could hold 3." (Seijoh 12, Tokyo 6.) **He left the eye alone.** Rallies: Kuroo into Bokuto's block 0-1; Lev serve into the net 0-2; **Sho passes Akaashi's float (feet first), Lev's tight set, Sho takes the arm off and rolls it deep; Akaashi digs it (saw the arm come off); long rally; Bokuto through Lev's hands into the curtain (2nd dent), 0-3. (No out-of-system kill: saved for ch 43-44.)** Late on Hinata every time ("HA!"); rotated to the back; sat on the floor laughing ("Lev asked if he was all right"). **2 Bokuto digs at the edge of Kuroo's shadow; 0 blocks; 1 of 4 swings in. Owls won 15-9;** Bokuto's aeroplane lap. **Kuroo: "You're different tonight, notebook." "Am I?" "Dunno."**
- **Daichi at 10:15: "Lights out was at 10."** Hinata: "I did a feint! A real 1! Over Tsukishima!" "Tell me tomorrow. Bed... You too, Kiyose." Walk back: Hinata's invisible feint; Tsukishima: "We saw." Tomorrow (day 8): **Shinzen, Nekoma, Ubugawa, Fukurodani last. Karasuno winless all week.**
- **Futons:** "Tomorrow. We're going to win 1." "Shinzen's 1st." "We've never beaten Shinzen." "I know." Tanaka sleep-talking: "That's my spot... rice."

- Next: ch 41 "Four into one" (Sho): day 8, game 1 vs Shinzen: **Karasuno's 1st win of the camp, 25-23 (user approved flip)**; Sho as the all-rounder (the dig, the call, the serve at the right man); eye and map agree on Shinzen's setter; **Tanaka finishes it.** 1 line of the ace.

## Chapter 41: 4 into 1

On the last morning Yaku served 10 at him at 6:05, and Sho passed 10, and Yaku served the 11th at Lev's face and went on serving at Lev's face for the rest of the half hour.

At breakfast Hinata ate 4 bowls of rice and said "Shinzen" between every 1, the way he'd said "tempo" 3 weeks ago, as if the word might run off if he stopped holding it.

At 8:15 Ukai read the lineup in the corner of the main gym.

"Kageyama. Hinata. Tsukishima. Daichi. Tanaka. Nishinoya." He paused. "Kiyose, Asahi's spot. Whole set."

Asahi nodded at once. His back had been stiff since the 6th day, from 3 days of jump serves, and he'd told Ukai about it at dinner, which a year ago, Suga said later, he'd never have done. He put his big hand on Sho's shoulder on the way to the bench.

"Um," said Asahi. "Borrow it."

"Thank you, senpai."

Tanaka was standing beside Sho with his arms folded and his chin up. He'd started every game all week except the 1 on the 6th day, when he'd sat on the bench with the paddle across his knees and watched somebody else play his spot.

"Same team today," said Tanaka. His eyes stayed on Shinzen, warming up across the net. "We've never beaten them."

"I know, senpai."

"Not once. Not in the whole history of the whole camp. Ennoshita asked their coach."

"He laughed," said Ennoshita, from the bench. "Kindly."

Tanaka's jaw went tight. "There's no kind way to laugh at somebody," he said, and turned round and walked out onto the court as if he were going to fight it.

---

Shinzen was plain.

That was the word Sho had for them, after a week. Nekoma read you. Fukurodani overpowered you. Ubugawa served you off the court. Shinzen did the ordinary things, receive and set and hit and block and cover, very well, every time, in the same order, without fuss, and waited for you to make a mistake. Against a team that was trying a lot of new things at once, which Karasuno was, that was usually enough.

They'd beaten Karasuno twice already that week. On the 6th day Ukai had banned the new quick in the middle of 1 of those games, and Shinzen had seen the old 1 often enough by now that their middle had started guessing it.

Sho had watched their setter on Nekomata's tape and from the bench all week. A 3rd year with tidy hands and a calm face, who set the ball the same way every time and liked to set his ace on the left when he was in trouble. The ace was tall and hit flat and hard. The right-side hitter was smaller and smarter. The libero was quick and cheated toward the ace, a step, every time the ace was in the front row.

He had all 6 of them in his head before the 1st whistle, 3 more than last night, and every 1 of them was plain.

---

The 1st point was Shinzen's. The 2nd was Shinzen's.

Their ace hit the 3rd ball into the floor between Daichi and Sho, a flat hard cross that Sho had crossed out the line for and stood in the right strip for, and was still a half step late on, because the ace hit earlier than his approach said he would.

"Sorry," said Sho.

"Mine," said Daichi, at the same time, and they both stood there and watched the ball roll.

Nishinoya, on his knees behind them, looked from 1 to the other and burst out laughing. "Somebody take it next time!"

0-3.

Sho went back to his spot and looked at the ace again. Earlier than his approach said. He put it in his head next to everything else. The next time the ace came in from the left, Sho moved half a beat sooner, feet 1st, Yaku's feet, while he was still looking, and the ball came at his chest, and he was there. It went up to Kageyama, soft. Kageyama set Tanaka, and Tanaka hit it into Shinzen's floor so hard the sound came back off the roof.

1-3.

"YEAH!" said Tanaka. He turned round and found Sho and pointed at him, both arms. "NICE PASS!"

---

It went the way games against Shinzen went: slowly, 1 point at a time, never more than 2 apart.

Sho served at 3-4 and sent his float at Shinzen's right-side hitter, the smaller smarter 1, in the front row, so he'd have to pass and then hit. The right-side hitter passed it, and was late getting out to hit, and Shinzen's setter went to the ace instead, and Tsukishima, who had been waiting all week for the ace to come to him, waited 1 beat longer than the ace expected, and went up late and soft, and the ball came off his hands and went straight up into Karasuno's court. Nishinoya dug it, and Kageyama put it back over on 2, and it dropped.

4-4.

"NICE, TSUKISHIMA!" Noya shouted.

Tsukishima pushed his glasses up, and on the bench Yamaguchi's whole face went bright.

On the next rally Kageyama set Hinata, the normal quick, the 1 Coach had said, and Hinata hit it, eyes shut, the old way, and it went in. Hinata landed scowling at his own hand, and then grinning at it.

5-5.

In the back row Sho called the short 1s. "Short!" when Shinzen's setter dumped, and Daichi was there. "Tip!" once, over Hinata, and Noya was there.

In the front row he blocked Shinzen's left side, their ace, and waited, the way he'd learned to, and twice the ball came off his hands up into his own court, and once it went straight down, a stuff, the 1st block he'd ever put straight down at a camp, and Tanaka slapped the back of his head so hard he saw stars.

"GOOD!" Tanaka yelled. "THAT'S GOOD! DO IT AGAIN!"

He hit, too. It was his 3rd whole set on the wing, and Kageyama set him 6 times, on the right, and he hit 4 of them in, high hands and a full swing, and 1 he tooled off the end blocker on the outside, and 1 went into the net because he ran under it. Shinzen started putting 2 blockers on him when he was in the front row. The 3rd time they did it, Kageyama saw it, and set the middle instead, and Hinata was there.

13-13.

---

At 13-13 Shinzen's coach changed something, and Sho saw it at once, because it was the kind of thing he'd have put in a clip for a coach by 8 in the morning.

Shinzen's libero stopped cheating toward the ace.

All game he'd been taking a step toward the ace's side whenever the ace was in the front row, the way he had all week. Now he stood square, in the middle of the back row, the way a libero stands when somebody has told him *they've noticed you leaning.*

Sho looked at Ukai, on the bench.

Ukai was already looking at him. He lifted 2 fingers off his folded arm, very slightly, and lowered them again.

Sho nodded back and went to his spot.

---

They were level at 15, and at 18, and at 21.

At 21-21 Tsukishima went up for a quick and Shinzen's middle went up with him, early, guessing, and got his hand on it, and the ball went straight up off his fingers. Daichi dug it. Kageyama set Tanaka, and Tanaka was blocked, and Noya dug the block, flat on his back, and the ball went up high and wild toward the antenna.

Sho was at the net on the right. He watched it go up. It was a bad ball, too tight and drifting, the kind he wanted, and it was going to the left antenna, the whole width of the court away from him, and Tanaka was already running for it.

Tanaka took it at the antenna with 2 blockers in front of him and hit it straight through both of them, so hard that it went off the 2nd blocker's wrist and into the floor behind him.

22-21.

Tanaka landed, and spun round, and roared, a sound with no words in it, and the whole Karasuno bench came off the bench. At the net Sho found he'd shouted too, a sound with no words in it either, and didn't care.

---

Shinzen came back. They always came back. 22-22. 22-23, on a tip that dropped between Sho and Daichi, the same seam as the 3rd point, and this time they both went and nearly collided and neither got it. Shinzen's bench went up. Their ace stood at the net with his hands on his knees and breathed.

23-23, on a Tsukishima quick that Shinzen's middle had stopped guessing at.

The rotation brought Sho to the end line.

He served at the libero, square in the middle of the back row, and the libero passed it, a good pass, because he was a good libero. Shinzen's setter set the ace. Tsukishima got a hand on it; Daichi dug it; Kageyama set Tanaka on the left, and Tanaka hit it cross, hard, past the block and in.

24-23. Sho's serve again.

---

Shinzen's coach called a timeout.

Sho stood on the end line with the ball in his hands while the others went to the bench. He thought about the libero standing square now, in the middle of the back row. He thought about the right-side hitter, the smaller smarter 1, who'd rotated to the right back and was standing beside the libero in the passing line, a step closer to him than usual. 2 good passers, close together. And between them, the seam.

Tanaka came out of Karasuno's huddle before the whistle and walked down the side of the court to the end line, where he wasn't supposed to be, and stood next to Sho.

"Kiyose."

"Senpai."

"If it comes to me," said Tanaka, low, his eyes on the net, "I'm finishing it."

"Okay."

Tanaka nodded, once, and went back to his spot.

The whistle went.

---

Sho bounced the ball twice. He looked at the seam.

He tossed it and hit it, flat and slow and plain, his ordinary float that was worse than Yamaguchi's, and it went over the net and straight at the place between the libero and the right-side hitter, and both of them moved, and both of them stopped, for 1 beat, the beat of *mine* and *yours*, and then the libero went, a fraction late, and got it up, off the net, high and outside, a long way from the setter.

The setter ran.

Sho came in off the end line into the middle of the back row and stopped.

He knew enough of where Shinzen's setter could go from there. A ball that far off the net, high, outside, with the setter running: the quick was gone. You can't set a middle quick off a pass like that. The back set to the right was gone; the setter's shoulders were facing the wrong way and he'd be setting off 1 foot. The dump was gone; he was too far from the net. What was left was the ball a setter throws when he's in trouble. High, outside, to his ace, on the left. The 1 everybody can see coming.

Most of the court went dark. What was left was a shape.

At the net, Tsukishima had seen the same thing. He was already sliding out to the right, toward where Shinzen's ace was coming in, with Kageyama beside him. He went up late and soft with his hands angled, the way he'd learned on the 4th night. Sho moved to the edge of Tsukishima's shadow, the bit just off the outside hand, where Kuroo had told him the ball comes when a hitter tries to go round.

And the gym went quiet.

It came on its own. He hadn't asked. It came the way you feel somebody sit down beside you on a bench, and it was looking at the same thing he was looking at.

Shinzen's setter hung in the air under the ball in points of light, and the ghost peeled off his hands a blink early, and went high and outside to the left, to the ace.

The map had said the ace. The eye said the ace.

Then the ghost moved to the ace, peeled off his hand, and turned at the top, wrist rolling outside, soft, the way a clever hitter goes round a block that's waiting for him. The map knew where that went.

Sho was standing in front of it before the ace's hand touched the ball.

The noise came back. The ball came off Tsukishima's fingertips, exactly where the map had said a ball off Tsukishima's fingertips would come, and dropped into Sho's arms, and he gave with it, soft, Yaku's give, and it went up, high and slow, toward the net.

Toward Kageyama.

Kageyama was landing from the block, off balance, on the wrong foot. He wasn't going to make it. Sho saw that from the back row, the way he'd seen everything in the last 2 seconds: Kageyama was a step and a half from the ball and falling, and the ball was coming down 2 meters off the net with nobody under it.

Sho was already running.

"MINE!"

He got under it. His hands were up before it arrived, the small window between the thumbs and forefingers, the shape already there. He bent his knees and let the ball come down into the pads of his fingers, and stood up, and the ball went up because he stood up. Legs 1st, then the trunk, then the arms, the hands only finishing it, the way Akaashi had said, upside down, like hitting.

It went high and outside to the left. Ordinary: high enough, off the net enough, a ball a hitter could hit.

Tanaka was already coming in.

He came in from the left with his whole body, the big shouting approach he'd had since April, with Shinzen's 2 blockers lining up in front of him, and he went up into Sho's ordinary set as if it were the best set anybody had ever given him.

He hit it straight down past both of them, between their hands, into the floor.

25-23.

---

Nobody on the court moved.

Then Hinata screamed, high and enormous, with no words in it, and it went up into the roof, and then Hinata was running, and Noya was running, and the bench was off the bench, Yamaguchi and Ennoshita and Narita and Kinoshita and Asahi with his stiff back, all of them, and Suga, and Takeda was standing up in front of the bench with his hands over his face.

Tanaka had landed on the other side of the 3-meter line and was standing with his arms straight out, both fists clenched, his head back, his mouth open. No sound was coming out of it. Then a sound came out of it, and it was the loudest sound Sho had ever heard a human being make.

Sho stood in the middle of the back row and let his hands come down out of the shape of the window.

His right hand had stayed at his side all game. He noticed that, and said, under his breath, to the hand, anyway, the way Mio had told him to: "It's fine."

Then Tanaka hit him.

It was a hug, technically. It came in at full speed from the side and lifted Sho off his feet and swung him round, and Tanaka was shouting in his ear, "YOU SET ME! YOU SET ME THE BALL! I FINISHED IT! I SAID I'D FINISH IT!" and then Hinata hit them both from the other side and they all went down in a heap on the floor of Shinzen's main gym, with Noya landing on top, and Sho lay at the bottom with somebody's knee in his ribs and somebody's elbow in his ear, laughing so hard he couldn't breathe.

---

On the bench Ukai was sitting very still with his arms folded and the cigarette behind his ear. Sho saw him over Tanaka's shoulder, upside down, from the floor. Ukai's face wasn't doing anything at all. Then it did something, at the very corner of his mouth, and he looked down at his own knees, and stayed like that.

Across the court, Shinzen's players were lining up at the end line.

Sho got up out of the heap, slowly, and stood and watched them.

They went to the hill. They went up it, all of them, the whole Shinzen team, running, while the rest of the camp watched from the gym door. Karasuno stood in a row on the grass and watched another team run their hill for the 1st time all week, and nobody cheered, because you don't, and Hinata held Sho's sleeve with 1 hand the whole time as if he might float off if he didn't.

At the top Shinzen's ace stopped and turned round and looked back down the hill at them, at Tanaka, and raised 1 hand.

Tanaka raised 1 back.

---

Nekoma were next. They were already warming up on the near court, red shirts in the morning light.

Sho walked back toward the gym with the others, and at the door, where the shade started, somebody was sitting on the step with a game console in his lap.

Kenma looked up.

His eyes went to Sho's face. Then, as always, down to his feet.

"Next is us," said Kenma.

"I know."

Kenma kept looking at Sho's feet. Sho was standing in the doorway, easy, with his heels where they always were.

"You still stop," said Kenma. "In a circle now."

Then he got up, and put the console in his pocket, and went in to warm up.
